result = Customers.CALCULATE(
    cust_name=name,
    num_total_transactions=COUNT(transactions_made),
    num_successful_transactions=COUNT(transactions_made.WHERE(status == "Completed"))
).WHERE(

).CALCULATE(
    customer_name=cust_name,
    success_rate=(num_successful_transactions * 100.0) / num_total_transactions
).ORDER_BY(
    success_rate.ASC()
)

# Calculate total and successful transactions per customer
customer_transaction_stats = Customers.CALCULATE(
    cust_name=name,
    total_txns=COUNT(transactions_made),
    successful_txns=COUNT(transactions_made.WHERE(status == "completed"))
)

# Filter for customers with at least 5 transactions
customers_with_min_transactions = customer_transaction_stats.WHERE(total_txns >= 5)

# Calculate success rate and select final fields, then order
customer_success_rates = customers_with_min_transactions.CALCULATE(
    customer_name=cust_name,
    success_rate=(successful_txns * 100.0) / total_txns
).ORDER_BY(success_rate.ASC())

[DEBUG] [Q2] Code generated by gemini:
# Define the list of FAANG ticker symbols
faang_symbols_list = ("AMZN", "AAPL", "GOOGL", "META", "NFLX")

# Filter customers who have a .com email address and have made transactions in FAANG stocks.
# - ENDSWITH(email, ".com") checks if the customer's email ends with ".com".
# - HAS(transactions_made.WHERE(ISIN(ticker.symbol, faang_symbols_list))) == 1 checks if the customer
#   has at least one transaction where the ticker symbol is in the faang_symbols_list.
#   - transactions_made is the sub-collection of transactions for each customer.
#   - ticker.symbol accesses the symbol of the ticker associated with a transaction.
#   - ISIN(ticker.symbol, faang_symbols_list) filters transactions to include only FAANG stocks.
selected_customers = Customers.WHERE(
    ENDSWITH(email, ".com") &
    (HAS(transactions_made.WHERE(ISIN(ticker.symbol, faang_symbols_list))) == 1)
)

# Calculate the total count of such distinct customers.
# The Broker collection is used as the top-level context for this global aggregation.
result = Broker.CALCULATE(
    num_distinct_faang_customers_with_com_email = COUNT(selected_customers)
)

# Filter transactions for the last 8 weeks excluding current week
transactions_last_8_weeks = Transactions.WHERE(
    (DATEDIFF("weeks", date_time, DATETIME("now")) >= 1) &
    (DATEDIFF("weeks", date_time, DATETIME("now")) <= 8)
).CALCULATE(
    transaction_id=transaction_id,
    week_start=DATETIME(date_time, "start of week"),
    is_weekend=(DAYOFWEEK(date_time) == 1) | (DAYOFWEEK(date_time) == 7)  # Saturday=7, Sunday=1
)

# Group by week and calculate counts
result = transactions_last_8_weeks.PARTITION(name="weeks", by=week_start).CALCULATE(
    week_start=week_start,
    total_transactions=COUNT(Transactions),
    weekend_transactions=SUM(Transactions.is_weekend)
).ORDER_BY(week_start.ASC())

[DEBUG] [Q4] Code generated by gemini:
# Define the start of the current week and the start of the 8-week target period.
# These values are calculated once at the Broker level and made available downstream.
weekly_stock_transaction_summary = Broker.CALCULATE(
    current_week_start_val = DATETIME('now', 'start of week'),
    target_period_start_val = DATETIME(DATETIME('now', 'start of week'), '-8 weeks')
).Transactions.WHERE(
    # Filter for stock transactions
    (ticker.ticker_type == 'stock') &
    # Filter for transactions within the last 8 weeks, excluding the current week
    (date_time >= target_period_start_val) &
    (date_time < current_week_start_val)
).CALCULATE(
    # Determine the starting date of the week for each transaction for grouping
    transaction_week_label = DATETIME(date_time, 'start of week'),
    # Flag if the transaction occurred on a weekend (Saturday or Sunday)
    is_on_weekend = ISIN(DAYNAME(date_time), ("Saturday", "Sunday"))
).PARTITION(
    # Group transactions by the week they occurred in
    name="weekly_groups", by=(transaction_week_label)
).CALCULATE(
    # Output the week identifier (start date of the week)
    week_starting_on = transaction_week_label,
    # Count total stock transactions for this week
    total_stock_txns = COUNT(Transactions),
    # Count stock transactions that occurred on a weekend for this week
    weekend_stock_txns = SUM(Transactions.is_on_weekend)
).ORDER_BY(week_starting_on.ASC())
Named columns are not supported in table alias.

[DEBUG] [Q4] DataFrame from claude is valid
[DEBUG] [Q4] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Calculate necessary information for each transaction from the Broker's Transactions collection
transactions_with_info = Broker.Transactions.CALCULATE(
    transaction_datetime_col = date_time,
    ticker_type_col = ticker.ticker_type,  # Access ticker_type from the related Ticker
    # Calculate how many full weeks ago this transaction occurred relative to the start of the current week.
    # A value of 1 means last week, 2 means the week before last, etc.
    weeks_ago_calculated = DATEDIFF("weeks", date_time, DATETIME('now', 'start of week')),
    # Flag for weekend transactions (1 if weekend, 0 if weekday)
    is_weekend_calculated = IFF(ISIN(DAYNAME(date_time), ("Saturday", "Sunday")), 1, 0)
)

# Step 2: Filter for stock transactions in the last 8 weeks (excluding the current week)
filtered_relevant_transactions = transactions_with_info.WHERE(
    (ticker_type_col == 'stock') &
    (weeks_ago_calculated >= 1) &  # Excludes current week (weeks_ago_calculated = 0)
    (weeks_ago_calculated <= 8)   # Includes up to 8 weeks ago
)

# Step 3: Group by week and calculate the required counts
# The PARTITION function groups the filtered_relevant_transactions by the weeks_ago_calculated value.
# For each group (each week), we then calculate the total and weekend transaction counts.
weekly_stock_transaction_counts = filtered_relevant_transactions.PARTITION(
    name="weekly_group", by=(weeks_ago_calculated)  # Partition by the calculated 'weeks_ago' value
).CALCULATE(
    week_past_identifier = weeks_ago_calculated,  # This is the 'Nth week ago' identifier (1-8)
    total_stock_txns = COUNT(Transactions),      # Counts all stock transactions in this weekly group
    weekend_stock_txns = SUM(is_weekend_calculated) # Sums the is_weekend_calculated flag (0 or 1) for transactions in this group
).ORDER_BY(week_past_identifier.ASC())  # Order results from most recent week (1) to oldest (8)

result = weekly_stock_transaction_counts

[DEBUG] [Q5] Code generated by claude:
# Calculate last week's date range (exclusive of current week)
last_week_transactions = Customers.WHERE(country == "USA").transactions_made.WHERE(
    (DATEDIFF("days", DATETIME("now", "start of week", "-7 days"), date_time) >= 0) &
    (DATEDIFF("days", date_time, DATETIME("now", "start of week", "-1 day")) >= 0)
)

result = Broker.CALCULATE(
    num_transactions=COUNT(last_week_transactions),
    total_amount=SUM(last_week_transactions.amount)
)

[DEBUG] [Q5] Code generated by gemini:
# First, we define a collection of transactions that meet the specified criteria.
# This involves calculating the date boundaries for "last week (exclusive of the current week)".
# - '_start_of_current_week' is the beginning of the current week (e.g., Monday 00:00:00).
# - '_start_of_last_week' is the beginning of the week prior to the current week.
# These date boundaries are calculated within the context of the 'Broker' collection and become available
# for filtering its sub-collections, like 'Transactions'.
filtered_txns_for_calc = Broker.CALCULATE(
    _start_of_current_week = DATETIME('now', 'start of week'),
    _start_of_last_week = DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-7 days')
).Transactions.WHERE( # Access the 'Transactions' sub-collection of 'Broker'
    (Transactions.customer.country == "USA") &  # Filter for transactions by customers in the USA
    (Transactions.date_time >= _start_of_last_week) &  # Transaction date must be on or after the start of last week
    (Transactions.date_time < _start_of_current_week)  # Transaction date must be before the start of the current week
)

# Now, we calculate the final result using the 'filtered_txns_for_calc' collection.
# We count the number of transactions and sum their 'amount'.
# The result is a single row from the 'Broker' collection context.
result = Broker.CALCULATE(
    num_transactions = COUNT(filtered_txns_for_calc),
    total_transaction_amount = SUM(filtered_txns_for_calc.amount)
)

# - '_start_of_last_week' is the beginning of the week prior to the current week.
# These date boundaries are calculated within the context of the 'Broker' collection and become available
# for filtering its sub-collections, like 'Transactions'.
filtered_txns_for_calc = Broker.CALCULATE(
    _start_of_current_week = DATETIME('now', 'start of week'),
    _start_of_last_week = DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-7 days')
).Transactions.WHERE( # Access the 'Transactions' sub-collection of 'Broker'
    (Transactions.customer.country == "USA") &  # Filter for transactions by customers in the USA
    (Transactions.date_time >= _start_of_last_week) &  # Transaction date must be on or after the start of last week
    (Transactions.date_time < _start_of_current_week)  # Transaction date must be before the start of the current week
)

# Now, we calculate the final result using the 'filtered_txns_for_calc' collection.
# We count the number of transactions and sum their 'amount'.
# The result is a single row from the 'Broker' collection context.
result = Broker.CALCULATE(
    num_transactions = COUNT(filtered_txns_for_calc),
    total_transaction_amount = SUM(filtered_txns_for_calc.amount)
)

[DEBUG] [Q7] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Filter transactions for "buy" type and within the last 10 days (today + 9 previous days),
# and calculate the ticker symbol for partitioning.
filtered_buy_transactions = Transactions.WHERE(
    (transaction_type == "buy") &
    (DATEDIFF("days", date_time, DATETIME('now')) < 10) # Covers 10 days: 0 (today) to 9 days ago
).CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol_val=ticker.symbol # Extract ticker symbol for grouping
    # transaction_id is implicitly available for COUNT(Transactions) to work on distinct transactions
)

# Step 2: Group by ticker symbol and count the number of buy transactions in each group.
# 'Transactions' in COUNT(Transactions) refers to the records from the 'Transactions' collection
# that satisfy the conditions of 'filtered_buy_transactions' and belong to the current partition group.
ticker_transaction_counts = filtered_buy_transactions.PARTITION(
    name="ticker_groups", by=(ticker_symbol_val)
).CALCULATE(
    ticker_s=ticker_symbol_val, # The ticker symbol from the partition key
    num_buys=COUNT(Transactions) # Count of buy transactions for this ticker symbol
)

# Step 3: Select the top 2 ticker symbols based on the number of buy transactions.
top_2_frequent_tickers = ticker_transaction_counts.TOP_K(
    2, by=num_buys.DESC()
)

# Step 4: Format the final output to return the ticker symbol and the count of buy transactions.
result = top_2_frequent_tickers.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=ticker_s,
    number_of_buy_transactions=num_buys
)

# Define the date range: last 6 months excluding current month
recent_customers = Customers.WHERE(
    MONOTONIC(
        DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-6 months'),
        join_date,
        DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-1 day')
    )
).CALCULATE(
    customer_id=_id,
    join_month=JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(join_date), LPAD(MONTH(join_date), 2, "0"))
)

# Calculate PMCS (per month customer signups)
pmcs_data = recent_customers.PARTITION(name="months", by=join_month).CALCULATE(
    month=join_month,
    pmcs=COUNT(Customers)
)

# Calculate PMAT (per month average transaction amount) for these customers
customer_transactions = recent_customers.transactions_made.CALCULATE(
    transaction_month=JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(date_time), LPAD(MONTH(date_time), 2, "0")),
    transaction_amount=amount
)

pmat_data = customer_transactions.PARTITION(name="tx_months", by=transaction_month).CALCULATE(
    month=transaction_month,
    pmat=AVG(Transactions.amount)
)

# Combine the results
result = pmcs_data.CALCULATE(
    month=month,
    pmcs=pmcs,
    pmat=DEFAULT_TO(pmat_data.WHERE(pmat_data.month == month).SINGULAR().pmat, 0)
).ORDER_BY(month.ASC())

# Define the date range: last 6 months excluding current month
recent_customers = Customers.WHERE(
    MONOTONIC(
        DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-6 months'),
        join_date,
        DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-1 day')
    )
)

    signup_month=JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(join_date), LPAD(MONTH(join_date), 2, "0"))
)

    signup_month=signup_month,
    pmcs=COUNT(Customers)
)

# Calculate PMAT for each month - average transaction amount per customer per month

    signup_month=signup_month,
    transaction_month=JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(transactions_made.date_time), LPAD(MONTH(transactions_made.date_time), 2, "0")),
    transaction_amount=transactions_made.amount
)

# Group by signup month and transaction month to get average transaction amounts
pmat_data = customer_transactions.PARTITION(name="signup_months", by=signup_month).PARTITION(name="transaction_months", by=transaction_month).CALCULATE(
    signup_month=signup_month,
    transaction_month=transaction_month,
    pmat=AVG(Customers.transactions_made.amount)
)

# Combine the results
result = pmcs_data.CALCULATE(
    signup_month=signup_month,
    pmcs=pmcs,
    pmat=AVG(pmat_data.WHERE(signup_month == signup_month).pmat)
).ORDER_BY(signup_month.ASC())

# Define the start of the current month and the start of the 6-month period prior to it.
# The period effectively covers the 6 full months preceding the current month.
current_month_start_date = DATETIME('now', 'start of month')
six_months_ago_start_date = DATETIME(current_month_start_date, '-6 months')

# Filter customers who joined within the last 6 months (excluding the current month)
# and extract their signup year and month.
customers_in_period = Customers.WHERE(
    (join_date >= six_months_ago_start_date) & (join_date < current_month_start_date)
).CALCULATE(
    signup_year_val=YEAR(join_date),
    signup_month_val=MONTH(join_date)
    # transactions_made will be accessed in the next step
)

# Partition the filtered customers by their signup year and month,
# then calculate PMCS and PMAT for each group.
monthly_metrics = customers_in_period.PARTITION(
    name="monthly_signup_group", by=(signup_year_val, signup_month_val)
).CALCULATE(
    output_signup_year=signup_year_val,
    output_signup_month=signup_month_val,
    pmcs_calc=COUNT(Customers),  # PMCS: Per Month Customer Signups
    pmat_calc=AVG(Customers.transactions_made.amount)  # PMAT: Per Month Average Transaction Amount
)

# Format the signup month string and select the final fields, ordering by month.
result = monthly_metrics.CALCULATE(
    signup_month_str=JOIN_STRINGS("-", output_signup_year, LPAD(output_signup_month, 2, "0")),
    pmcs=pmcs_calc,
    pmat=pmat_calc
).ORDER_BY(output_signup_year.ASC(), output_signup_month.ASC())

# Define the date range: last 6 months excluding current month
recent_customers = Customers.WHERE(

customer_info = recent_customers.CALCULATE(

        DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-1 day')
    )
).CALCULATE(

    amount=amount
)

    transaction_month=transaction_month,
    pmat=AVG(Transactions.amount)
)

result = pmcs_data.CALCULATE(

[DEBUG] [Q8] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Define the time boundaries for the last 6 months, excluding the current month.
# 'start_current_month_val' is the first day of the current month.
# 'start_six_months_ago_val' is the first day of the month, 6 months prior to the current month.
time_boundaries_calc = Broker.CALCULATE(
    start_current_month_val=DATETIME('now', 'start of month'),
    start_six_months_ago_val=DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-6 months')
)

# Step 2 & 3: Filter customers who joined within the defined period and extract signup year/month.
customers_in_signup_period = time_boundaries_calc.Customers.WHERE(
    (join_date >= start_six_months_ago_val) & (join_date < start_current_month_val)
).CALCULATE(
    # Carry forward customer data, and add signup year and month
    # _id, name, email, etc. are implicitly available if needed for 'transactions_made'
    signup_year_val=YEAR(join_date),
    signup_month_num_val=MONTH(join_date)
    # transactions_made sub-collection is implicitly carried forward
)

# Step 4: Group customers by their signup year and month.
# Step 5: Calculate PMCS (Per Month Customer Signups) and PMAT (Per Month Average Transaction Amount).
monthly_metrics = customers_in_signup_period.PARTITION(
    name="signup_monthly_group", by=(signup_year_val, signup_month_num_val)
).CALCULATE(
    # These are the partition keys
    grouped_signup_year=signup_year_val,
    grouped_signup_month_num=signup_month_num_val,
    # PMCS: Count of customers who signed up in this specific month.
    pmcs_calculated=COUNT(customers_in_signup_period),
    # PMAT: Average transaction amount for all transactions made by customers who signed up in this month.
    # If a customer group has no transactions, AVG will typically return null or handle as per DB behavior.
    pmat_calculated=AVG(customers_in_signup_period.transactions_made.amount)
)

# Step 6: Format the output month string, select final fields, and order by year and month.
result = monthly_metrics.CALCULATE(
    signup_month_display=JOIN_STRINGS("-", grouped_signup_year, LPAD(grouped_signup_month_num, 2, "0")),
    PMCS=pmcs_calculated,
    PMAT=pmat_calculated
).ORDER_BY(grouped_signup_year.ASC(), grouped_signup_month_num.ASC())

[DEBUG] [Q10] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Filter customers who joined in 2022
customers_joined_2022 = Customers.WHERE(YEAR(join_date) == 2022)

# Step 2: Calculate necessary flags for partitioning and aggregation.
# 'cust_country_key' will be used as the partitioning key.
# 'customer_has_txn_flag' is true if the customer (who joined in 2022) has transactions.
# 'customer_is_active_and_has_txn_flag' is true if the customer (who joined in 2022) is active and has transactions.
customer_intermediate_data = customers_joined_2022.CALCULATE(
    cust_country_key = country,
    customer_has_txn_flag = HAS(transactions_made) == 1,
    customer_is_active_and_has_txn_flag = (status == "Active") & (HAS(transactions_made) == 1)
)

# Step 3 & 4: Partition by country and aggregate to get counts for AR calculation.
# 'country_partition_group' refers to the collection of customer records within each country partition.
# We sum the boolean flags (which act as 1 for true, 0 for false) to get the counts.
country_level_summary = customer_intermediate_data.PARTITION(
    name="country_partition_group", by=(cust_country_key)
).CALCULATE(
    output_country_name = cust_country_key, # The country name from the partition key
    num_total_with_transactions = SUM(country_partition_group.customer_has_txn_flag),
    num_active_with_transactions = SUM(country_partition_group.customer_is_active_and_has_txn_flag)
)

# Step 5: Calculate the Activity Ratio (AR).
# IFF is used to prevent division by zero if a country has no customers with transactions.
ar_calculation = country_level_summary.CALCULATE(
    country_final_name = output_country_name,
    activity_ratio_value = IFF(
        num_total_with_transactions > 0,
        (100.0 * num_active_with_transactions) / num_total_with_transactions,
        0.0
    )
)

# Step 6: Select the final fields: country and AR.
result = ar_calculation.CALCULATE(country = country_final_name, AR = activity_ratio_value)

# Step 1: Filter customers who joined in 2022
customers_joined_2022 = Customers.WHERE(YEAR(join_date) == 2022)

# Step 2: For these customers, calculate flags for having transactions and being active, and get their country
customer_details_for_ar = customers_joined_2022.CALCULATE(
    cust_country_attr = country,  # Attribute for partitioning
    has_transactions_flag = HAS(transactions_made) == 1,
    is_active_customer_flag = status == "Active" # Assuming 'Active' status string
)

# Each group in the partition will contain customers from a specific country who joined in 2022
country_partitioned_data = customer_details_for_ar.PARTITION(name="country_customer_group", by=(cust_country_attr))

# Step 4: Calculate AR for each country
# Within each country_customer_group:
# - Numerator: Count customers who are active AND have transactions.
# - Denominator: Count customers who have transactions.
ar_calculation_by_country = country_partitioned_data.CALCULATE(
    country_output_name = cust_country_attr,
    # Count active customers with transactions within this country group
    num_active_with_transactions = COUNT(country_customer_group.Customers.WHERE(is_active_customer_flag == 1 & has_transactions_flag == 1)),
    # Count total customers with transactions within this country group
    total_customers_with_transactions = COUNT(country_customer_group.Customers.WHERE(has_transactions_flag == 1)),
    # Calculate Activity Ratio, handling division by zero
    activity_ratio_value = IFF(
        total_customers_with_transactions > 0,
        (100.0 * num_active_with_transactions) / total_customers_with_transactions,
        0.0  # AR is 0 if no customers with transactions
    )
)

# Step 5: Select the final required fields: country and AR
result = ar_calculation_by_country.CALCULATE(country=country_output_name, AR=activity_ratio_value)

# Step 1: Filter customers who joined in 2022 and calculate flags for AR components.
# 'cust_country_attr' stores the country for partitioning.
# 'is_active_with_txns_flag' is true if customer is active and has transactions.
# 'has_txns_flag' is true if customer has transactions.
customers_joined_2022_prepared = Customers.WHERE(YEAR(join_date) == 2022).CALCULATE(
    cust_country_attr=country,
    is_active_with_txns_flag=((status == 'Active') & (HAS(transactions_made) == 1)),
    has_txns_flag=(HAS(transactions_made) == 1)
)

# Step 2: Partition by country and sum the flags to get counts for numerator and denominator.
# 'output_country_name' will be the country name from the partition key.
# 'num_for_numerator' is the sum of 'is_active_with_txns_flag' for each country.
# 'num_for_denominator' is the sum of 'has_txns_flag' for each country.
country_level_aggregates = customers_joined_2022_prepared.PARTITION(
    name="country_partition_groups", by=(cust_country_attr)
).CALCULATE(
    output_country_name=cust_country_attr,
    num_for_numerator=SUM(customers_joined_2022_prepared.is_active_with_txns_flag),
    num_for_denominator=SUM(customers_joined_2022_prepared.has_txns_flag)
)

# Step 3: Calculate the Activity Ratio (AR) for each country.
# AR = (Number of Active Customers with Transactions / Total Number of Customers with Transactions) * 100.
# If the denominator is 0, AR is set to 0.0.
activity_ratio_by_country = country_level_aggregates.CALCULATE(
    country=output_country_name,
    AR=IFF(
        num_for_denominator > 0,
        (num_for_numerator * 100.0) / num_for_denominator,
        0.0
    )
)

# Filter sell transactions from the past month

    (transaction_type == "sell") &
    (DATEDIFF("days", date_time, DATETIME('now')) <= 30)
)

# Group by ticker symbol and calculate SPM
result = sell_transactions_past_month.PARTITION(name="ticker_groups", by=ticker_id).CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=ticker.symbol,
    total_amount=SUM(Transactions.amount),
    total_costs=SUM(Transactions.tax) + SUM(Transactions.commission),

[DEBUG] [Q12] DataFrame from claude is None
[DEBUG] [Q12] Code generated by gemini:
# Define the start of the period (1 month ago from now)
one_month_ago_timestamp = DATETIME('now', '-1 month')

# Start with Tickers, calculate relevant sell transactions for each
tickers_data = Tickers.CALCULATE(
    ticker_s=symbol,
    # Filter transactions_of for sell transactions in the last month
    sell_tx_last_month=transactions_of.WHERE(
        (transaction_type == "sell") & (date_time >= one_month_ago_timestamp)
    )
)

# Filter out tickers that have no such sell transactions
tickers_with_sells = tickers_data.WHERE(
    HAS(sell_tx_last_month) == 1
)

# Calculate aggregate values needed for SPM for the filtered tickers
aggregated_data = tickers_with_sells.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol_out=ticker_s,
    total_amount_from_sells=SUM(sell_tx_last_month.amount),
    total_tax_from_sells=SUM(sell_tx_last_month.tax),
    total_commission_from_sells=SUM(sell_tx_last_month.commission)
)

# Calculate SPM using the aggregated values
spm_calculated_data = aggregated_data.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol_final=ticker_symbol_out,
    # SPM formula: (Total Amount - (Tax + Commission)) / Total Amount * 100
    # Handle division by zero: if Total Amount is 0, SPM is 0.0
    calculated_spm=IFF(
        total_amount_from_sells == 0,
        0.0, # SPM is a percentage, so float
        (total_amount_from_sells - (total_tax_from_sells + total_commission_from_sells)) * 100.0 / total_amount_from_sells
    )
)

# Select the final required fields: ticker symbol and SPM
result = spm_calculated_data.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=ticker_symbol_final,
    spm=calculated_spm
)

[DEBUG] [Q12] Code generated by gemini:
# Define the start date for the "past month, inclusive of 1 month ago"
# This means from the start of the day, one month prior to today, up to the current time.
start_date_val = DATETIME('now', 'start of day', '-1 month')

# Step 1: Filter transactions to include only 'sell' transactions within the defined date range.
# We start from Broker.Transactions as Transactions is a top-level collection under Broker.
filtered_sell_transactions = Broker.Transactions.WHERE(
    (transaction_type == "sell") & (date_time >= start_date_val)
)

# Step 2: Partition the filtered sell transactions by ticker symbol.
# For each ticker symbol, calculate the sum of amount, tax, and commission.
# 'ticker.symbol' is accessible because Transactions has a 'ticker' relationship to the Tickers collection, which has a 'symbol' field.
# Inside CALCULATE, 'Transactions.amount', 'Transactions.tax', and 'Transactions.commission' refer to the
# transactions within the current partition group (i.e., for a specific ticker_symbol).
aggregated_data_by_ticker = filtered_sell_transactions.PARTITION(name="sells_by_ticker", by=(ticker.symbol)).CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol_value = ticker.symbol,  # The partition key
    total_sell_amount = SUM(Transactions.amount),
    total_sell_tax = SUM(Transactions.tax),
    total_sell_commission = SUM(Transactions.commission)
)

# Step 3: Calculate the Selling Profit Margin (SPM) for each ticker symbol.
# The SPM formula is (Total Amount from Sells - (Tax + Commission)) / Total Amount from Sells * 100.
# We use IFF to handle cases where total_sell_amount might be zero to prevent division by zero errors.
spm_calculated_per_ticker = aggregated_data_by_ticker.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol_output = ticker_symbol_value,
    calculated_spm = IFF(
        total_sell_amount == 0,
        0.0,  # If total sell amount is 0, SPM is 0.0 (or None, if preferred)
        (total_sell_amount - (total_sell_tax + total_sell_commission)) * 100.0 / total_sell_amount
    )
)

# Step 4: Select the final required fields: ticker symbol and SPM.
result = spm_calculated_per_ticker.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol = ticker_symbol_output,
    SPM = calculated_spm
)

# Filter transactions for sell type in the past month
sell_transactions_past_month = Transactions.WHERE(
    (transaction_type == "sell") &
    (DATEDIFF("days", date_time, DATETIME('now')) <= 30)
)

# Group by ticker symbol and calculate SPM
result = sell_transactions_past_month.PARTITION(name="ticker_groups", by=ticker_id).CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=ticker.symbol,
    total_amount=SUM(Transactions.amount),
    total_costs=SUM(Transactions.CALCULATE(cost=tax + commission).cost),
    spm=((SUM(Transactions.amount) - SUM(Transactions.CALCULATE(cost=tax + commission).cost)) / SUM(Transactions.amount)) * 100
)

[DEBUG] [Q12] Code generated by gemini:
# Define the start of the period (1 month ago from now)
one_month_ago_timestamp = DATETIME('now', '-1 month')

# Start with Tickers, calculate relevant sell transactions for each
tickers_data = Tickers.CALCULATE(
    ticker_s=symbol,
    # Filter transactions_of for sell transactions in the last month
    sell_tx_last_month=transactions_of.WHERE(
        (transaction_type == "sell") & (date_time >= one_month_ago_timestamp)
    )
)

# Filter out tickers that have no such sell transactions
tickers_with_sells = tickers_data.WHERE(
    HAS(sell_tx_last_month) == 1
)

# Calculate aggregate values needed for SPM for the filtered tickers
aggregated_data = tickers_with_sells.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol_out=ticker_s,
    total_amount_from_sells=SUM(sell_tx_last_month.amount),
    total_tax_from_sells=SUM(sell_tx_last_month.tax),
    total_commission_from_sells=SUM(sell_tx_last_month.commission)
)

# Calculate SPM using the aggregated values
spm_calculated_data = aggregated_data.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol_final=ticker_symbol_out,
    # SPM formula: (Total Amount - (Tax + Commission)) / Total Amount * 100
    # Handle division by zero: if Total Amount is 0, SPM is 0.0
    calculated_spm=IFF(
        total_amount_from_sells == 0,
        0.0, # SPM is a percentage, so float
        (total_amount_from_sells - (total_tax_from_sells + total_commission_from_sells)) * 100.0 / total_amount_from_sells
    )
)

# Select the final required fields: ticker symbol and SPM
result = spm_calculated_data.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=ticker_symbol_final,
    spm=calculated_spm
)

# Step 1: Extract month and ticker symbol information from DailyPrices
price_info = DailyPrices.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=ticker.symbol,
    year=YEAR(date),
    month=MONTH(date),
    year_month=JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(date), LPAD(MONTH(date), 2, "0"))
)

# Step 2: Partition by ticker symbol and year_month to calculate monthly statistics
monthly_stats = price_info.PARTITION(name="monthly_groups", by=(ticker_symbol, year_month)).CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=ticker_symbol,
    year=year,
    month=month,
    year_month=year_month,
    avg_closing_price=AVG(DailyPrices.close),
    highest_price=MAX(DailyPrices.high),
    lowest_price=MIN(DailyPrices.low)
)

# Step 3: Calculate MoMC using PREV function to get previous month's average
result = monthly_stats.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=ticker_symbol,
    month=year_month,
    avg_closing_price=avg_closing_price,
    highest_price=highest_price,
    lowest_price=lowest_price,
    MoMC=IFF(
        PREV(avg_closing_price, by=year_month.ASC(), per="ticker_symbol") != None,
        (avg_closing_price - PREV(avg_closing_price, by=year_month.ASC(), per="ticker_symbol")) / 
        PREV(avg_closing_price, by=year_month.ASC(), per="ticker_symbol"),
        None
    )
).ORDER_BY(ticker_symbol.ASC(), year_month.ASC())

# Step 1: Prepare daily price data with necessary fields (ticker symbol, year, month, close, high, low)
price_data_with_time_parts = DailyPrices.CALCULATE(
    calc_ticker_symbol=ticker.symbol,
    calc_year=YEAR(date),
    calc_month=MONTH(date),
    calc_close_price=close,
    calc_high_price=high,
    calc_low_price=low
)

# Step 2: Partition by ticker symbol, year, and month to calculate monthly aggregates
monthly_aggregated_data = price_data_with_time_parts.PARTITION(
    name="monthly_price_group", by=(calc_ticker_symbol, calc_year, calc_month)
).CALCULATE(
    key_ticker_symbol=calc_ticker_symbol,
    key_year=calc_year,
    key_month=calc_month,
    agg_avg_close_price=AVG(DailyPrices.close),
    agg_max_high_price=MAX(DailyPrices.high),
    agg_min_low_price=MIN(DailyPrices.low)
)

# Step 3: Partition the monthly aggregated data by ticker symbol to set up context for PREV function
ticker_partitioned_monthly_data = monthly_aggregated_data.PARTITION(
    name="ticker_group_for_momc", by=(key_ticker_symbol)
)

# Step 4: Access the sub-collection of monthly data per ticker and calculate previous month's average close price
# The sub-collection is named 'monthly_price_group' (the name of the partition in step 2).
data_with_prev_close = ticker_partitioned_monthly_data.monthly_price_group.CALCULATE(
    out_ticker_symbol=key_ticker_symbol, # Field from the sub-collection monthly_price_group
    out_year=key_year,                   # Field from the sub-collection monthly_price_group
    out_month=key_month,                 # Field from the sub-collection monthly_price_group
    out_avg_close_price=agg_avg_close_price, # Field from the sub-collection monthly_price_group
    out_max_high_price=agg_max_high_price,   # Field from the sub-collection monthly_price_group
    out_min_low_price=agg_min_low_price,     # Field from the sub-collection monthly_price_group
    calc_prev_month_avg_close=PREV(
        agg_avg_close_price,
        by=(key_year.ASC(), key_month.ASC()),
        per="ticker_group_for_momc", # Ensures PREV is calculated per ticker
        default=None
    )
)

# Step 5: Calculate MoMC using the previous month's average close and format the output
ticker_monthly_financial_summary = data_with_prev_close.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=out_ticker_symbol,
    month=JOIN_STRINGS("-", TEXT(out_year), LPAD(TEXT(out_month), 2, "0")),
    average_closing_price=out_avg_close_price,
    highest_price=out_max_high_price,
    lowest_price=out_min_low_price,
    MoMC=IFF(
        calc_prev_month_avg_close == None,
        None,
        (out_avg_close_price - calc_prev_month_avg_close) / calc_prev_month_avg_close
    )
).ORDER_BY(ticker_symbol.ASC(), month.ASC())

# Step 1: Extract month information and ticker symbol from DailyPrices
price_info = DailyPrices.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=ticker.symbol,

)

)

[DEBUG] [Q13] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Prepare daily price details with year, month, and ticker symbol
price_details = DailyPrices.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol_val=ticker.symbol,
    year_val=YEAR(date),
    month_val=MONTH(date),
    year_month_str_val=JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(date), LPAD(MONTH(date), 2, "0"))
    # DailyPrices.close, DailyPrices.high, DailyPrices.low are implicitly available for aggregation
)

# Step 2: Outer partition by ticker symbol. This creates the 'ticker_level_data' context.
ticker_partition = price_details.PARTITION(
    name="ticker_level_data", # Name for the ticker-level grouping, used by PREV's 'per'
    by=(ticker_symbol_val)
)

# Step 3: Inner partition by month on the sub-collection of daily prices for each ticker.
# Then calculate monthly aggregates for each ticker.
# ticker_partition.price_details refers to the 'price_details' records for the current ticker group.
monthly_data_for_ticker = ticker_partition.price_details.PARTITION(
    name="month_level_data", # Name for the month-level grouping within each ticker
    by=(year_val, month_val, year_month_str_val) # Partition keys from price_details
).CALCULATE(
    # Carry over ticker symbol from the outer partition's scope
    ticker_symbol_calc=ticker_symbol_val,
    # Carry over year, month, and year_month_str from the inner partition's keys
    year_calc=year_val,
    month_calc=month_val,
    year_month_str_calc=year_month_str_val,
    # Calculate monthly aggregates using original DailyPrices fields within the group
    avg_close_price_calc=AVG(DailyPrices.close),
    max_high_price_calc=MAX(DailyPrices.high),
    min_low_price_calc=MIN(DailyPrices.low)
)

# Step 4: Calculate PREV (previous month's average close) on the monthly aggregated data.
# This operates on 'monthly_data_for_ticker', which is effectively per-ticker due to the structure.
# PREV uses 'per="ticker_level_data"' to ensure it's calculated within each ticker group.
data_with_prev_close = monthly_data_for_ticker.CALCULATE(
    # Carry over all necessary fields
    ticker_symbol_carry=ticker_symbol_calc,
    year_carry=year_calc,
    month_carry=month_calc,
    year_month_str_carry=year_month_str_calc,
    avg_close_price_carry=avg_close_price_calc,
    max_high_price_carry=max_high_price_calc,
    min_low_price_carry=min_low_price_calc,

    # Calculate previous month's average closing price
    prev_avg_close_val=PREV(
        avg_close_price_calc, # Expression to get previous value of
        by=(year_calc.ASC(), month_calc.ASC()), # Order within each ticker group for PREV
        per="ticker_level_data", # Specifies that PREV operates per group of the 'ticker_level_data' partition
        default=None # Value if no previous record (e.g., first month for a ticker)
    )
)

# Step 5: Calculate MoMC and select final output fields
final_result_unsorted = data_with_prev_close.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=ticker_symbol_carry,
    month=year_month_str_carry, # Use the "YYYY-MM" string format for month
    average_closing_price=avg_close_price_carry,
    highest_price=max_high_price_carry,
    lowest_price=min_low_price_carry,
    # Calculate MoMC, handling cases where prev_avg_close_val is None or zero
    momc=IFF(
        (prev_avg_close_val == None) | (prev_avg_close_val == 0),
        None, # MoMC is not applicable or undefined
        (avg_close_price_carry - prev_avg_close_val) / prev_avg_close_val
    ),
    # Keep original year and month for sorting if they are not part of the final SELECT
    year_for_sort=year_carry,
    month_for_sort=month_carry
)

# Step 6: Sort the final result
ticker_monthly_momc_analysis = final_result_unsorted.ORDER_BY(
    ticker_symbol.ASC(),
    year_for_sort.ASC(),
    month_for_sort.ASC()
).CALCULATE( # Final projection to include only the requested columns
    ticker_symbol=ticker_symbol,
    month=month,
    average_closing_price=average_closing_price,
    highest_price=highest_price,
    lowest_price=lowest_price,
    momc=momc
)

# Filter daily prices for April 1-4, 2023
april_prices = DailyPrices.WHERE(
    (date >= "2023-04-01") & (date <= "2023-04-04")
)

    ticker_symbol=DailyPrices.ticker.symbol,
    max_high=MAX(DailyPrices.high),

)

# Step 1: Filter DailyPrices for the specified date range
filtered_daily_prices = DailyPrices.WHERE((date >= "2023-04-01") & (date <= "2023-04-04"))

# Step 2 & 3: Partition by ticker_id and calculate symbol, max high, min low, and price difference for each stock
stock_price_analysis = filtered_daily_prices.PARTITION(name="prices_per_stock", by=(ticker_id)).CALCULATE(
    # Get the ticker symbol. DailyPrices.ticker.symbol refers to the symbol for each daily price entry in the group.
    # Since ticker_id is fixed for the group, ticker.symbol is also fixed. MIN selects this common symbol.
    ticker_symbol_value=MIN(DailyPrices.ticker.symbol),
    # Calculate the price difference: (max high price in period) - (min low price in period)
    # MAX(DailyPrices.high) aggregates over the 'DailyPrices' sub-collection for the current partition group.
    price_difference=(MAX(DailyPrices.high) - MIN(DailyPrices.low))
)

# Step 4: Select the top 3 stocks with the highest price difference
top_3_stock_changes = stock_price_analysis.TOP_K(3, by=price_difference.DESC())

# Step 5: Select and rename fields for the final output
result = top_3_stock_changes.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=ticker_symbol_value,
    price_change=price_difference
)

result = Tickers.CALCULATE(
    # Create intermediate fields for ticker symbol and its filtered historical prices
    ticker_sym_intermediate = symbol,
    filtered_prices_intermediate = historical_prices.WHERE((date >= "2023-04-01") & (date <= "2023-04-04"))
).WHERE(
    # Ensure that there are historical prices for the ticker in the specified date range
    HAS(filtered_prices_intermediate) == 1
).CALCULATE(
    # Carry forward the ticker symbol
    ticker_sym_intermediate = ticker_sym_intermediate,
    # Calculate the price difference: (max high price in period) - (min low price in period)
    price_diff_intermediate = MAX(filtered_prices_intermediate.high) - MIN(filtered_prices_intermediate.low)
).TOP_K(
    # Select the top 3 tickers based on the calculated price difference in descending order
    3, by=price_diff_intermediate.DESC()
).CALCULATE(

# Filter daily prices for April 1-4, 2023
april_prices = DailyPrices.WHERE(
    (date >= "2023-04-01") & (date <= "2023-04-04")
)

# Group by ticker and calculate price change for each stock
stock_price_changes = april_prices.PARTITION(name="stocks", by=ticker_id).CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=ticker.symbol,
    max_high=MAX(DailyPrices.high),
    min_low=MIN(DailyPrices.low),
    price_change=MAX(DailyPrices.high) - MIN(DailyPrices.low)
)

# Get the top 3 stocks with highest price change
result = stock_price_changes.TOP_K(3, by=price_change.DESC()).CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=ticker_symbol,
    price_change=price_change
)

result = Tickers.CALCULATE(
    # Store the ticker symbol and filter its historical prices for the specified date range
    ts_val = symbol,
    relevant_prices_subcollection = historical_prices.WHERE((date >= "2023-04-01") & (date <= "2023-04-04"))
).WHERE(
    # Ensure the ticker has price data within the range
    HAS(relevant_prices_subcollection) == 1
).CALCULATE(
    # Carry over the ticker symbol
    ticker_symbol_output = ts_val,
    # Calculate the maximum high and minimum low price across the filtered period
    max_overall_high = MAX(relevant_prices_subcollection.high),
    min_overall_low = MIN(relevant_prices_subcollection.low)
).CALCULATE(
    # Carry over the ticker symbol again
    ticker_symbol_output = ticker_symbol_output,
    # Calculate the price difference
    price_diff = max_overall_high - min_overall_low
).TOP_K(
    # Select the top 3 tickers by the calculated price difference in descending order
    3, by=price_diff.DESC()
).CALCULATE(
    # Select the final fields: ticker symbol and the price change
    ticker_symbol = ticker_symbol_output,
    price_change = price_diff
)

    join_month=MONTH(join_date),

    (date_time >= "2023-01-01") & (date_time <= "2023-03-31")
)

    name="transaction_types", 
    by=transaction_type
).CALCULATE(
    transaction_type=transaction_type,
    distinct_customers=NDISTINCT(Transactions.customer_id),

# Step 1: Filter transactions by the specified date range.
# We convert date_time to the start of the day to ensure accurate date comparisons.
filtered_transactions = Transactions.WHERE(
    (DATETIME(date_time, 'start of day') >= "2023-01-01") &
    (DATETIME(date_time, 'start of day') <= "2023-03-31")
)

# Step 2: Partition the filtered transactions by transaction_type.
# For each type, calculate the number of distinct customers and the average number of shares.
transaction_type_summary = filtered_transactions.PARTITION(
    name="type_groups", by=(transaction_type)
).CALCULATE(
    transaction_type=transaction_type,  # The transaction type from the partition key
    num_distinct_customers=NDISTINCT(Transactions.customer_id),  # Count distinct customers for this transaction type
    avg_shares=AVG(Transactions.shares)  # Calculate average shares for this transaction type
)

# Step 3: Select the top 3 transaction types based on the number of distinct customers.
# The result will include transaction_type, num_distinct_customers, and avg_shares.
result = transaction_type_summary.TOP_K(
    3, by=num_distinct_customers.DESC()
)

# Step 1: Filter transactions to the specified date range.
# We use explicit timestamp boundaries to ensure inclusivity of the start and end dates.
filtered_transactions = Transactions.WHERE(
    (date_time >= "2023-01-01T00:00:00.000") & (date_time <= "2023-03-31T23:59:59.999")
)

# Step 2: Group by transaction_type and calculate the required metrics.
# - 'transaction_type' is the partitioning key.
# - 'num_distinct_customers' counts unique customer_id values within each group from the filtered Transactions.
# - 'avg_num_shares' calculates the average of shares within each group from the filtered Transactions.
transaction_summary = filtered_transactions.PARTITION(name="txn_groups", by=(transaction_type)).CALCULATE(
    type_of_transaction = transaction_type,
    num_distinct_customers = NDISTINCT(Transactions.customer_id),
    avg_num_shares = AVG(Transactions.shares)
)

# Step 3: Select the top 3 transaction types based on the number of distinct customers.
# The results are ordered in descending order of num_distinct_customers.
top_3_transaction_types_by_customer_count = transaction_summary.TOP_K(3, by=num_distinct_customers.DESC())

# The variable 'top_3_transaction_types_by_customer_count' now holds the desired result.
result = top_3_transaction_types_by_customer_count

# Step 1: Calculate customer state and ticker type for each transaction
transaction_info = Transactions.CALCULATE(
    cust_s=customer.state,
    tick_t=ticker.ticker_type
)

# Step 2: Partition by customer state and ticker type, then count transactions for each combination
state_ticker_combinations = transaction_info.PARTITION(
    name="combinations_group", by=(cust_s, tick_t)
).CALCULATE(
    customer_state=cust_s,
    ticker_type=tick_t,
    num_transactions=COUNT(Transactions)
)

# Step 3: Select the top 5 combinations by the number of transactions
top_5_combinations_by_transactions = state_ticker_combinations.TOP_K(5, by=num_transactions.DESC())

[DEBUG] [Q26] DataFrame from claude is valid
[DEBUG] [Q26] Code generated by gemini:
# Filter transactions to the last 30 days (inclusive)
recent_transactions = Transactions.WHERE(
    (DATEDIFF("days", date_time, DATETIME('now')) <= 30) & (date_time <= DATETIME('now'))
)

# Add customer country and transaction amount to the filtered transactions
# customer.country gives the country of the customer who made the transaction
transactions_with_country_details = recent_transactions.CALCULATE(
    cust_country = customer.country,
    transaction_value = amount
)

# Partition by customer country
country_grouped_transactions = transactions_with_country_details.PARTITION(
    name="country_transactions_group",  # Name for the partition group
    by=(cust_country)
)

# Calculate aggregates for each country: number of transactions and total transaction amount
country_aggregates = country_grouped_transactions.CALCULATE(
    country_name = cust_country,  # The country name is the partition key
    num_of_transactions = COUNT(transactions_with_country_details),  # Count transactions within this country's partition
    total_transaction_amount = SUM(transactions_with_country_details.transaction_value)  # Sum transaction amounts within this country's partition
)

# Get the top 5 countries by total transaction amount
top_countries_by_transaction_amount = country_aggregates.TOP_K(
    5,
    by=total_transaction_amount.DESC()
)

result = top_countries_by_transaction_amount

[DEBUG] [Q26] DataFrame from claude is valid
[DEBUG] [Q26] Code generated by gemini:
# 1. Filter transactions to include only those from the last 30 days.
# DATEDIFF("days", date_time, 'now') <= 30 means transactions from today up to 30 days ago.
recent_transactions = Transactions.WHERE(DATEDIFF("days", date_time, 'now') <= 30)

# 2. For these recent transactions, extract the customer's country and the transaction amount.
# 'customer.country' accesses the country of the customer associated with the transaction.
# 'tx_amount' is created to hold the transaction amount for clarity in aggregation.
transactions_info = recent_transactions.CALCULATE(
    cust_country=customer.country,
    tx_amount=amount
)

# 3. Partition the processed transactions by customer country.
# This groups transactions by 'cust_country'.
# Then, for each country, calculate the total transaction amount and the number of transactions.
# - 'country_name' takes the value of 'cust_country' from the partition key.
# - 'SUM(Transactions.tx_amount)' sums the 'tx_amount' for all transactions within the current country partition.
# - 'COUNT(Transactions)' counts the number of transactions within the current country partition.
country_level_summary = transactions_info.PARTITION(
    name="country_group", by=(cust_country)
).CALCULATE(
    country_name=cust_country,
    total_transaction_amount=SUM(Transactions.tx_amount),
    num_of_transactions=COUNT(Transactions)
)

# 4. Select the top 5 countries by total transaction amount in descending order.
# Then, format the output to include the country name, number of transactions, and total transaction amount.
top_countries_by_transaction_amount = country_level_summary.TOP_K(
    5, by=total_transaction_amount.DESC()
).CALCULATE(
    country_name=country_name,
    num_of_transactions=num_of_transactions,
    total_transaction_amount=total_transaction_amount
)
DATEDIFF unsupported for 'DAYS'.

# Filter transactions for Q1 2023 and successful status
q1_2023_transactions = Transactions.WHERE(
    (YEAR(date_time) == 2023) &
    (MONTH(date_time) >= 1) &
    (MONTH(date_time) <= 3) &
    (status == "SUCCESS")  # Assuming "SUCCESS" indicates successful transactions
).CALCULATE(
    month = MONTH(date_time),
    transaction_price = price
)

# Group by month and calculate average price
result = q1_2023_transactions.PARTITION(name="months", by=month).CALCULATE(
    month = month,
    avg_transaction_price = AVG(Transactions.price)
).ORDER_BY(month.ASC())

# Filter transactions for Q1 2023, successful status, and extract the transaction month
transactions_in_q1_2023_with_month = Transactions.WHERE(
    (YEAR(date_time) == 2023) &
    (MONTH(date_time) >= 1) &
    (MONTH(date_time) <= 3) &
    (status == "successful")  # Assuming 'successful' is the value for successful status
).CALCULATE(
    transaction_month = MONTH(date_time)
    # The 'price' field from Transactions is implicitly carried forward
)

# Partition by month and calculate the average transaction price for each month
monthly_average_prices = transactions_in_q1_2023_with_month.PARTITION(
    name="monthly_group", by=(transaction_month)
).CALCULATE(
    month = transaction_month,  # Output the month
    average_transaction_price = AVG(Transactions.price)  # Calculate average price for the group
).ORDER_BY(month.ASC()) # Order results by month

result = monthly_average_prices

[DEBUG] [Q32] Running models for: For sales with sale price over $30,000, how many payments were received in total and on weekends in each of the last 8 calendar weeks (excluding the current week)? Return the week (as a date), total payments received, and weekend payments received in ascending order.

[DEBUG] [Q32] Code generated by claude:
# Get the date range for last 8 weeks (excluding current week)
date_info = Dealership.CALCULATE(
    current_week_start=DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-0 weeks'),
    eight_weeks_ago=DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-9 weeks')
)

# Get payments from high-value sales in the target date range
high_value_payments = date_info.Sales.WHERE(sale_price > 30000).payment.WHERE(
    (payment_date >= eight_weeks_ago) & 
    (payment_date < current_week_start)
).CALCULATE(
    week_start=DATETIME(payment_date, 'start of week'),
    is_weekend=ISIN(DAYOFWEEK(payment_date), (1, 7))
)

# Group by week and calculate totals
result = high_value_payments.PARTITION(name="weeks", by=week_start).CALCULATE(
    week=week_start,
    total_payments=COUNT(PaymentsReceived),
    weekend_payments=SUM(PaymentsReceived.is_weekend)
).ORDER_BY(week.ASC())
[DEBUG] [Q32] DataFrame from claude is None
[DEBUG] [Q32] Code generated by gemini:
# Define date boundaries for the analysis period.
# 'analysis_start_dt' is the start of the week, 8 weeks prior to the current week's start.
# 'analysis_end_dt' is the end of the last full week (i.e., one day before the current week's start).
date_boundaries = Dealership.CALCULATE(
    # Using DATETIME('now', 'start of week') to get the start of the current week.
    # Then, subtract 8 weeks to find the start of the 8-week-ago period.
    analysis_start_dt = DATETIME(DATETIME('now', 'start of week'), '-8 weeks'),
    # End of the analysis period is one day before the start of the current week.
    analysis_end_dt = DATETIME(DATETIME('now', 'start of week'), '-1 day')
)

# 1. Filter sales with sale_price > $30,000.
# 2. Access their associated payments. The date_boundaries are available from the context.
payments_from_high_value_sales = date_boundaries.Sales.WHERE(sale_price > 30000.0).payment

# 3. Filter these payments to include only those within the last 8 full calendar weeks.
# 4. For each relevant payment, determine its week's start date and if it occurred on a weekend.
payments_in_analysis_period = payments_from_high_value_sales.WHERE(
    (payment_date >= analysis_start_dt) & (payment_date <= analysis_end_dt)
).CALCULATE(
    week_start_for_payment = DATETIME(payment_date, 'start of week'),
    is_payment_on_weekend = ISIN(DAYNAME(payment_date), ("Saturday", "Sunday"))
)

# 5. Group the filtered payments by their week's start date.
#    For each week, calculate the total number of payments and the number of weekend payments.
weekly_payment_stats = payments_in_analysis_period.PARTITION(
    name="payments_grouped_by_week", by=(week_start_for_payment)
).CALCULATE(
    week_iso_date = week_start_for_payment,  # The partition key, representing the start of the week.
    total_payments_in_week = COUNT(payments_in_analysis_period), # Total payments in this week's group.
    weekend_payments_in_week = SUM(IFF(payments_in_analysis_period.is_payment_on_weekend, 1, 0)) # Sum of weekend payments (1 if true, 0 if false).
).ORDER_BY(week_iso_date.ASC()) # 6. Order results by week in ascending order.

result = weekly_payment_stats

# Get payments from high-value sales in the target date range
high_value_payments = date_info.Sales.WHERE(sale_price > 30000).payment.WHERE(
    (payment_date >= eight_weeks_ago) & 
    (payment_date < current_week_start)
).CALCULATE(
    week_start=DATETIME(payment_date, 'start of week'),
    is_weekend=ISIN(DAYOFWEEK(payment_date), (1, 7))
)

# Group by week and calculate totals
result = high_value_payments.PARTITION(name="weeks", by=week_start).CALCULATE(
    week=week_start,
    total_payments=COUNT(PaymentsReceived),
    weekend_payments=SUM(PaymentsReceived.is_weekend)
).ORDER_BY(week.ASC())
[DEBUG] [Q32] DataFrame from claude is None
[DEBUG] [Q32] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Define date boundaries at the top level Dealership collection.
# - current_week_start_dt: The start date of the current week.
# - start_date_for_analysis_calc: The start date of the week 8 weeks prior to the current week's start.
# - end_date_for_analysis_calc: The end date of the last full week (day before current_week_start_dt).
date_info = Dealership.CALCULATE(
    current_week_start_dt=DATETIME('now', 'start of week'),
    start_date_for_analysis_calc=DATETIME(DATETIME('now', 'start of week'), '-8 weeks'),
    end_date_for_analysis_calc=DATETIME(DATETIME('now', 'start of week'), '-1 day')
)

# Step 2: Identify relevant payments from sales over $30,000 within the defined date range.
# For each payment, calculate its week's start date and if it was made on a weekend.
payments_in_scope = date_info.Sales.WHERE(sale_price > 30000).payment.WHERE(
    (payment_date >= start_date_for_analysis_calc) & (payment_date <= end_date_for_analysis_calc)
).CALCULATE(
    payment_week_start_date_calc=DATETIME(payment_date, 'start of week'),
    is_weekend_payment_calc=ISIN(DAYNAME(payment_date), ("Saturday", "Sunday"))
)

# Step 3: Group payments by their calculated week_start_date and aggregate counts.
# For each week, count total payments and the subset of those made on weekends.
weekly_summary = payments_in_scope.PARTITION(name="payments_per_week", by=(payment_week_start_date_calc)).CALCULATE(
    week_as_date=payment_week_start_date_calc,
    total_payments_received_count=COUNT(PaymentsReceived),
    weekend_payments_received_count=SUM(IFF(is_weekend_payment_calc, 1, 0))
)

# Step 4: Order the weekly summaries by week_as_date in ascending order.
result = weekly_summary.ORDER_BY(week_as_date.ASC())

# The LIKE function with UPPER ensures case-insensitivity and partial matching.
cars_filtered_by_vin = Dealership.Cars.WHERE(LIKE(UPPER(vin_number), '%M5%'))

# Partition the filtered cars by their make and model.
# For each unique make/model group, calculate the make, model, and the total number of sales.
result = cars_filtered_by_vin.PARTITION(
    name="model_group",  # Assigns a name to the collection of groups created by the partition
    by=(make, model)     # Specifies the fields to partition by (car make and model)
).CALCULATE(
    car_make=make,       # Selects the 'make' from the partition key
    car_model=model,     # Selects the 'model' from the partition key
    num_sales=COUNT(Cars.sale_records)  # Counts the number of sales records for cars within the current make/model group
)

# Step 1: Calculate aggregated sales data for each salesperson.
# This includes their first name, last name, the sum of their sales (total_sales_amount),
# and the count of sales they made (number_of_sales).
sales_data_per_salesperson = Salespersons.CALCULATE(
    sp_fn=first_name,  # Temporary alias for first_name
    sp_ln=last_name,   # Temporary alias for last_name
    agg_total_sales=SUM(sales_made.sale_price),  # Sum of sale_price from all sales_made by the salesperson
    agg_num_sales=COUNT(sales_made)              # Count of sales_made by the salesperson
)

# Step 2: Calculate the sales rank (SR) and format the final output.
# The SR is determined by ranking salespersons based on their agg_total_sales in descending order.
# The result is then ordered by SR in ascending order to show the top salesperson first.
final_result = sales_data_per_salesperson.CALCULATE(
    first_name=sp_fn,                        # Final field: first_name
    last_name=sp_ln,                         # Final field: last_name
    total_sales_amount=agg_total_sales,      # Final field: total_sales_amount
    number_of_sales=agg_num_sales,           # Final field: number_of_sales
    SR=RANKING(by=agg_total_sales.DESC())    # Calculate Sales Rank (SR)
).ORDER_BY(SR.ASC())                         # Order the results by SR (ascending)

    latest_inventory_status=inventory_snapshots.WHERE(
        RANKING(by=snapshot_date.DESC()) == 1
    ).SINGULAR().is_in_inventory,
    max_sale_price=MAX(sale_records.sale_price)
)

# Filter for cars no longer in inventory and group by make/model
result = cars_with_latest_inventory.WHERE(
    latest_inventory_status == False
).PARTITION(name="make_model_groups", by=(car_make, car_model)).CALCULATE(
    make=car_make,
    model=car_model,
    highest_sale_price=MAX(Cars.max_sale_price)
).ORDER_BY(highest_sale_price.DESC())

# Step 1: Start from the Sales collection. For each sale, retrieve information about the car,
# its make, model, and determine its latest inventory status.
sales_with_car_details = Sales.CALCULATE(
    # Store the sale price for later aggregation.
    sale_price_val=sale_price,
    # Get the make and model of the car involved in the sale.
    car_make_val=car.make,
    car_model_val=car.model,
    # Determine if the car is no longer in inventory based on its latest snapshot.
    # Access the car's inventory_snapshots.
    # RANKING(by=snapshot_date.DESC(), allow_ties=True) == 1 finds the latest snapshot(s) for this specific car.
    # .SINGULAR() selects one if multiple (e.g., same date, though is_in_inventory should be consistent).
    # Check if its is_in_inventory status is False.
    is_car_no_longer_in_inventory=(car.inventory_snapshots.WHERE(
        RANKING(by=snapshot_date.DESC(), allow_ties=True) == 1
    ).SINGULAR().is_in_inventory == False)
)

# Step 2: Filter these sales records to include only those where the car
# is confirmed to be no longer in inventory.
# Cars in the Sales collection are inherently "sold".
filtered_sales_for_cars_not_in_inventory = sales_with_car_details.WHERE(
    sales_with_car_details.is_car_no_longer_in_inventory == True
)

# Step 3: Group these filtered sales by car make and model.
# For each group, calculate the maximum sale price.
# The collection being partitioned is `filtered_sales_for_cars_not_in_inventory`.
# `MAX(filtered_sales_for_cars_not_in_inventory.sale_price_val)` will compute the max sale_price_val
# from the sales records within each (make, model) group.
max_sale_prices_by_make_model = filtered_sales_for_cars_not_in_inventory.PARTITION(
    name="make_model_group", by=(car_make_val, car_model_val)
).CALCULATE(
    # Output make and model from the partition keys.
    output_make=car_make_val,
    output_model=car_model_val,
    # Calculate the highest sale price for this group.
    output_highest_sale_price=MAX(filtered_sales_for_cars_not_in_inventory.sale_price_val)
)

# Step 4: Select the final required fields (make, model, highest_sale_price)
# and order the results by the highest sale price in descending order.
result = max_sale_prices_by_make_model.CALCULATE(
    make=output_make,
    model=output_model,
    highest_sale_price=output_highest_sale_price
).ORDER_BY(highest_sale_price.DESC())

[DEBUG] [Q37] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: For each sale, gather car's make, model, and its latest inventory status.
# Sales.car refers to the car associated with a given sale.
# Sales.car.inventory_snapshots provides the inventory history for that specific car.
# RANKING() without 'per' operates on the current collection (snapshots of the specific car).
sales_info = Sales.CALCULATE(
    sale_p=sale_price,  # Store the sale price
    car_mk=Sales.car.make,  # Get the make of the car involved in the sale
    car_mdl=Sales.car.model,  # Get the model of the car involved in the sale
    # Determine the latest inventory status for the car from its snapshots
    car_latest_status=Sales.car.inventory_snapshots.WHERE(
        RANKING(by=snapshot_date.DESC(), allow_ties=True) == 1  # Find the latest snapshot(s)
    ).SINGULAR().is_in_inventory  # Get the inventory status from one such latest snapshot
)

# Step 2: Filter sales to include only those where the car is no longer in inventory.
# A car is "sold" because it's in the Sales collection.
# "No longer in inventory" means car_latest_status is False.
# If car_latest_status is null (no snapshots) or True, the sale is excluded.
filtered_sales_records = sales_info.WHERE(
    car_latest_status == False
)

# Step 3: Group the filtered sales by car make and model.
# For each unique make/model combination, calculate the highest sale price.
make_model_max_prices = filtered_sales_records.PARTITION(
    name="sales_by_make_model", by=(car_mk, car_mdl)  # Group by make and model
).CALCULATE(
    make_name=car_mk,  # Output the make
    model_name=car_mdl,  # Output the model
    highest_sale_p=MAX(sale_p)  # Calculate the maximum sale price for this group
)

# Step 4: Order the results by the highest sale price, from highest to lowest.
final_result = make_model_max_prices.ORDER_BY(highest_sale_p.DESC())

)

    sale_month = DATETIME(sale_date, "start of month"),

).WHERE(

[DEBUG] [Q39] Code generated by gemini:
# Define date boundaries for the last 6 months, excluding the current month.
# current_month_start_dt_val will be the first day of the current month.
current_month_start_dt_val = DATETIME('now', 'start of month')
# six_months_ago_start_dt_val will be the first day of the month, 6 months prior to the current month.
# This marks the beginning of our 6-month window (inclusive).
six_months_ago_start_dt_val = DATETIME(current_month_start_dt_val, '-6 months')
# Sales will be filtered such that sale_date >= six_months_ago_start_dt_val and sale_date < current_month_start_dt_val,
# effectively covering the six full months before the current month.

# Step 1: Filter salespersons hired between 2022 and 2023 (inclusive).
# Access Salespersons collection from the Dealership graph.
hired_salespersons_filtered = Dealership.Salespersons.WHERE(
    (YEAR(hire_date) >= 2022) & (YEAR(hire_date) <= 2023)
)

# Step 2: Access their sales (sales_made), filter by the defined date range,
# and prepare month representations for grouping and sorting.
# sales_made is the sub-collection of Sales records associated with each salesperson.
sales_data_for_aggregation = hired_salespersons_filtered.sales_made.WHERE(
    (sale_date >= six_months_ago_start_dt_val) & (sale_date < current_month_start_dt_val)
).CALCULATE(
    # Keep original_sale_price to be summed later. This renames sale_price from the Sales collection
    # to avoid potential naming conflicts and make it clear what is being summed.
    original_sale_price = sale_price,
    # Create a datetime object representing the first day of the sale's month.
    # This will be used for sorting the final results chronologically.
    sale_month_datetime_key = DATETIME(sale_date, 'start of month'),
    # Create a 'YYYY-MM' string representation for the month.
    # This will be used as a partitioning key and for display.
    # LPAD ensures the month part is two digits (e.g., '01' for January).
    sale_month_label_key = JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(sale_date), LPAD(MONTH(sale_date), 2, "0"))
)

# Step 3: Partition the prepared sales data by month.
# Both sale_month_label_key (string 'YYYY-MM') and sale_month_datetime_key (datetime object)
# are used in the 'by' clause of PARTITION. This makes them available for the subsequent CALCULATE step
# (for selecting 'month') and the ORDER_BY step (for sorting).
# The partition name "sales_grouped_by_month" will refer to the collection of sales records (from sales_data_for_aggregation)
# belonging to each specific month group.
monthly_sales_groups = sales_data_for_aggregation.PARTITION(
    name="sales_grouped_by_month",
    by=(sale_month_label_key, sale_month_datetime_key)
)

# Step 4: Calculate the required metrics (PMSPS and PMSR) for each month group.
# PMSPS: Per Month Salesperson Sales count (number of sales in the month).
# PMSR: Per Month Sales Revenue (sum of sale_price for sales in the month).
# Order the results by month in ascending order using sale_month_datetime_key.
final_monthly_metrics = monthly_sales_groups.CALCULATE(
    month = sale_month_label_key,  # Display the 'YYYY-MM' month label, taken from the partition key.
    PMSPS = COUNT(sales_grouped_by_month),  # Count of sales records within this month's group.
    PMSR = DEFAULT_TO(SUM(sales_grouped_by_month.original_sale_price), 0.0)  # Sum of 'original_sale_price' for records in this group, defaulting to 0.0 if the sum is null.
).ORDER_BY(sale_month_datetime_key.ASC())

# Filter salespersons hired between 2022-2023 (inclusive)
eligible_salespersons = Salespersons.WHERE(
    (YEAR(hire_date) >= 2022) & (YEAR(hire_date) <= 2023)
)

# Get sales from eligible salespersons in the last 6 months excluding current month
sales_info = eligible_salespersons.sales_made.WHERE(
    (DATEDIFF("months", sale_date, DATETIME('now')) >= 1) &
    (DATEDIFF("months", sale_date, DATETIME('now')) <= 6)
).CALCULATE(
    sale_month=DATETIME(sale_date, 'start of month'),
    sale_price=sale_price
)

# Group by month and calculate metrics
monthly_metrics = sales_info.PARTITION(name="months", by=sale_month).CALCULATE(
    month=sale_month,
    PMSPS=COUNT(sales_made),
    PMSR=SUM(sales_made.sale_price)
)

# To ensure all 6 months are included (even with 0 values), we need to create a complete month range
# and left join with our metrics
result = monthly_metrics.CALCULATE(
    month=month,
    PMSPS=PMSPS,
    PMSR=PMSR
).ORDER_BY(month.ASC())

[DEBUG] [Q39] Code generated by gemini:
# Define Date Boundaries for the reporting period (last 6 full months, excluding current month)
current_month_start_dt = DATETIME('now', 'start of month')
# period_start_dt will be the first day of the month, 6 months ago.
# e.g., if current_month_start_dt is July 1st, period_start_dt will be January 1st.
period_start_dt = DATETIME(current_month_start_dt, '-6 months')
# period_end_exclusive_dt will be the first day of the current month.
# Sales up to, but not including, this date will be considered.
period_end_exclusive_dt = current_month_start_dt

# Step 1: Create a scaffold of all unique months within the desired reporting period.
# This is based on all sales in the system to ensure all calendar months with any sale are represented.
all_sales_with_month_info = Dealership.Sales.CALCULATE(
    sale_month_val = DATETIME(sale_date, 'start of month') # Truncate sale_date to the start of its month
)

# Get distinct months from all sales and filter them to the 6-month reporting window.
# These distinct months will form the rows of our report.
distinct_months_for_report = all_sales_with_month_info.PARTITION(
    name="month_partition", by=(sale_month_val) # Group by the calculated sale_month_val to get distinct months
).CALCULATE(
    report_month_value = sale_month_val # Select the distinct month value
).WHERE(
    (report_month_value >= period_start_dt) & (report_month_value < period_end_exclusive_dt) # Filter to the defined 6-month period
)

# Step 2: For each month in our scaffold, calculate PMSPS and PMSR.
# Metrics are specific to salespersons hired between 2022 and 2023.
monthly_metrics = distinct_months_for_report.CALCULATE(
    # The month being reported on
    Month = report_month_value,

    # PMSPS: Per Month Salesperson Sales count
    # Count sales made by salespersons hired in 2022-2023, where the sale_date (truncated to month) matches report_month_value.
    PMSPS = COUNT(
        Dealership.Salespersons.WHERE(
            (YEAR(hire_date) >= 2022) & (YEAR(hire_date) <= 2023) # Filter salespersons by hire year
        ).sales_made.WHERE(
            DATETIME(sale_date, 'start of month') == report_month_value # Filter their sales to the specific month from the scaffold
        )
    ),

    # PMSR: Per Month Sales Revenue
    # Sum of sale_price for the same set of filtered sales.
    PMSR = DEFAULT_TO( # Use DEFAULT_TO to ensure 0.0 if there are no sales, as SUM might return null.
        SUM(
            Dealership.Salespersons.WHERE(
                (YEAR(hire_date) >= 2022) & (YEAR(hire_date) <= 2023) # Filter salespersons by hire year
            ).sales_made.WHERE(
                DATETIME(sale_date, 'start of month') == report_month_value # Filter their sales to the specific month
            ).sale_price # Access the sale_price for summation
        ),
        0.0 # Default to 0.0 if the sum is null (i.e., no sales in that month for these salespersons)
    )
)

# Step 3: Order the results by month in ascending order.
final_report = monthly_metrics.ORDER_BY(Month.ASC())

).CALCULATE(

    name="month_partition", by=(sale_month_val) # Group by the calculated sale_month_val to get distinct months
).CALCULATE(
    report_month_value = sale_month_val # Select the distinct month value
).WHERE(
    (report_month_value >= period_start_dt) & (report_month_value < period_end_exclusive_dt) # Filter to the defined 6-month period
)

# Step 2: For each month in our scaffold, calculate PMSPS and PMSR.
# Metrics are specific to salespersons hired between 2022 and 2023.
monthly_metrics = distinct_months_for_report.CALCULATE(
    # The month being reported on
    Month = report_month_value,

    # PMSPS: Per Month Salesperson Sales count
    # Count sales made by salespersons hired in 2022-2023, where the sale_date (truncated to month) matches report_month_value.
    PMSPS = COUNT(
        Dealership.Salespersons.WHERE(
            (YEAR(hire_date) >= 2022) & (YEAR(hire_date) <= 2023) # Filter salespersons by hire year
        ).sales_made.WHERE(
            DATETIME(sale_date, 'start of month') == report_month_value # Filter their sales to the specific month from the scaffold
        )
    ),

    # PMSR: Per Month Sales Revenue
    # Sum of sale_price for the same set of filtered sales.
    PMSR = DEFAULT_TO( # Use DEFAULT_TO to ensure 0.0 if there are no sales, as SUM might return null.
        SUM(
            Dealership.Salespersons.WHERE(
                (YEAR(hire_date) >= 2022) & (YEAR(hire_date) <= 2023) # Filter salespersons by hire year
            ).sales_made.WHERE(
                DATETIME(sale_date, 'start of month') == report_month_value # Filter their sales to the specific month
            ).sale_price # Access the sale_price for summation
        ),
        0.0 # Default to 0.0 if the sum is null (i.e., no sales in that month for these salespersons)
    )
)

# Step 3: Order the results by month in ascending order.
final_report = monthly_metrics.ORDER_BY(Month.ASC())

# Find cars that were sold on the same day they went out of inventory
cars_sold_same_day = Cars.WHERE(
    HAS(sale_records) == 1 & HAS(inventory_snapshots) == 1
).CALCULATE(
    car_make=make,
    car_model=model,
    # Find the date when the car went out of inventory (is_in_inventory = false)
    inventory_out_date=inventory_snapshots.WHERE(is_in_inventory == False).snapshot_date,
    # Get the sale information
    sale_price=sale_records.sale_price,
    sale_date=sale_records.sale_date
).WHERE(
    # Filter for cars sold on the same day they went out of inventory
    sale_date == inventory_out_date
)

# Find the car with the highest sale price among those sold same day as inventory out
result = cars_sold_same_day.TOP_K(1, by=sale_price.DESC()).CALCULATE(
    make=car_make,
    model=car_model,
    sale_price=sale_price
)

[DEBUG] [Q43] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Filter sales to find those where the car was sold on the same day it went out of inventory.
# A car "went out of inventory" means an InventorySnapshot exists for that car
# with is_in_inventory = False on a certain snapshot_date.
# "Sold on the same day" means Sales.sale_date == InventorySnapshot.snapshot_date for that car.
candidate_sales = Sales.WHERE(
    HAS(
        # Access the car related to the current sale, then its inventory_snapshots
        car.inventory_snapshots.WHERE(
            # Condition 1: The snapshot_date must match the sale_date of the current Sale record
            (snapshot_date == sale_date) & \
            # Condition 2: The car must be marked as not in inventory in this specific snapshot
            (is_in_inventory == False)
        )
    ) == 1 # Ensures at least one such inventory snapshot exists for the car associated with the sale
)

# Step 2: From these candidate_sales, find the single sale with the highest sale_price.
highest_priced_sale = candidate_sales.TOP_K(1, by=sale_price.DESC())

# Step 3: Select the make, model of the car, and the sale_price from the identified sale.
# The car's make and model are accessed via the 'car' relationship from the Sale record.
result = highest_priced_sale.CALCULATE(
    make=car.make,
    model=car.model,
    sale_price=sale_price
)

[DEBUG] [Q44] Running models for: What is the total payments received per month? Also calculate the MoM change for each month.MoM change = (current month value - prev month value). Return all months in your answer, including those where there were no payments.
[DEBUG] [Q44] Running model claude (attempt 0)

[DEBUG] [Q44] Code generated by claude:
# Step 1: Extract month/year info and group payments by month
payment_info = PaymentsReceived.CALCULATE(
    year=YEAR(payment_date),
    month=MONTH(payment_date),
    year_month=JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(payment_date), LPAD(MONTH(payment_date), 2, "0"))
)

# Step 2: Group by year-month and calculate total payments per month
monthly_payments = payment_info.PARTITION(name="months", by=(year, month)).CALCULATE(
    year=year,
    month=month,
    year_month=year_month,
    total_payments=SUM(PaymentsReceived.payment_amount)
)

# Step 3: Calculate MoM change using PREV function
result = monthly_payments.CALCULATE(
    year=year,
    month=month,
    year_month=year_month,
    total_payments=total_payments,
    prev_month_payments=PREV(total_payments, by=(year.ASC(), month.ASC()), default=0),
    mom_change=total_payments - PREV(total_payments, by=(year.ASC(), month.ASC()), default=0)
).ORDER_BY(year.ASC(), month.ASC())
[DEBUG] [Q44] DataFrame from claude is None
[DEBUG] [Q44] Code generated by gemini:
# Calculate year and month for each payment
payments_with_year_month_info = Dealership.PaymentsReceived.CALCULATE(
    pay_year=YEAR(payment_date),
    pay_month=MONTH(payment_date),
    payment_amount=payment_amount # Carry forward payment_amount for SUM
)

# Partition by year and month, then calculate total payments and MoM change
monthly_payment_summary = payments_with_year_month_info.PARTITION(
    name="monthly_payments_group", by=(pay_year, pay_month)
).CALCULATE(
    year_val=pay_year,
    month_val=pay_month,
    total_monthly_payments=SUM(PaymentsReceived.payment_amount),
    mom_change_value=(
        SUM(PaymentsReceived.payment_amount) -
        PREV(SUM(PaymentsReceived.payment_amount), by=(pay_year.ASC(), pay_month.ASC()), default=0.0)
    )
).ORDER_BY(year_val.ASC(), month_val.ASC())

# Step 1: Extract month/year info and group payments by month
payment_info = PaymentsReceived.CALCULATE(
    year=YEAR(payment_date),
    month=MONTH(payment_date),
    year_month=JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(payment_date), LPAD(MONTH(payment_date), 2, "0"))
)

# Step 2: Group by year-month and calculate total payments per month
monthly_payments = payment_info.PARTITION(name="months", by=(year, month)).CALCULATE(
    year=year,
    month=month,
    year_month=year_month,
    total_payments=SUM(PaymentsReceived.payment_amount)
)

# Step 3: Calculate MoM change using PREV function
result = monthly_payments.CALCULATE(
    year=year,
    month=month,
    year_month=year_month,
    total_payments=total_payments,
    prev_month_payments=PREV(total_payments, by=(year.ASC(), month.ASC()), default=0),
    mom_change=total_payments - PREV(total_payments, by=(year.ASC(), month.ASC()), default=0)
).ORDER_BY(year.ASC(), month.ASC())

top_salespersons_by_asp = Dealership.Salespersons.CALCULATE(
    # Store original first and last names in new variables to carry them through
    sp_first_name=first_name,
    sp_last_name=last_name,
    # Calculate ASP for each salesperson
    # ASP = SUM(sales_made.sale_price) / COUNT(sales_made)
    # Use IFF to handle division by zero if a salesperson has no sales, setting ASP to 0.0
    asp_value=IFF(COUNT(sales_made) > 0, SUM(sales_made.sale_price) / COUNT(sales_made), 0.0)
).TOP_K(
    3,  # Get the top 3
    by=asp_value.DESC()  # Order by the calculated ASP in descending order
).CALCULATE(
    # Select the final fields as requested
    first_name=sp_first_name,
    last_name=sp_last_name,
    ASP=asp_value  # Rename asp_value to ASP for the final output
)

# First, process customers to gather sales data per state
state_level_sales_data = Customers.WHERE(
    HAS(car_purchases) == 1  # Consider only customers who have made purchases
).PARTITION(
    name="state_groups", by=(state)  # Group customers by their state
).CALCULATE(
    state_name=state,  # The state
    # Sum of sale_price from all purchases made by customers in this state
    total_revenue_for_state=SUM(Customers.car_purchases.sale_price),
    # Count of unique customer IDs in this state group
    num_unique_customers_for_state=NDISTINCT(Customers._id)
)

# Then, select the top 5 states based on total revenue
top_5_states_by_revenue = state_level_sales_data.TOP_K(
    5, by=total_revenue_for_state.DESC()  # Order by total revenue, descending
).CALCULATE(
    state=state_name,  # Output field: state
    num_unique_customers=num_unique_customers_for_state,  # Output field: number of unique customers
    total_revenue=total_revenue_for_state  # Output field: total revenue
)
